We invited some girls to come as well, and one of them used her evil ways to convince me to buy some alchahol . I bought a litre of Jack Daniels and a litre of Vodka. #169160 20 2006-04-28T04:55:59Z brocerius
Before i retort i would like to tell you a story. (Its true BTW)

As i said, i went through a religious phase that ended about a year ago. Every week i went to communion, every week i went to conformation classes, and i even started up a religous group for and by pupils, with two of my friends. I got baptized and then confirmed. During this time i was really extreme with religion. The group me and my friends started, called 'the Call', was really intense. We sang religous songs, discussed the bible, then we would sit aroud a table, holding hands, praying aloud for those we loved, and even those we hated. This probably sounds wierd to anyone who hasnt been there with that state of mind. It even sounds wierd to me reading it now. But that didnt matter then, because i felt a 'spiratual' connection. Im not saying i was connected with God. Im still not sure. But there was a great connection between the people that attended. We didnt hide our prayers in silence, we shared out thoughts and our deepest hopes. It was a truly great expirienc.

Then it just died. I lost the connection. I am still very close to the people that started it with me, but i dont feel the presence of God any more.

I know how stupid and corney this sounds, but i needed to let you know that im not just some idiot that thinks he knows all about god. I have been there and i have study it and felt it.


And i would also like to say that all the stuff i was saying erlyer about being born out of wedlock and going to hell, i dont actually belive that. I was just making a point. If i was to believe any part of the bible i would believe the New Testament, because it basically refutes all the teachings of the Old Testament. It says love you enemys, not kill them.

However, i dont believe that Jesus was God. I believe he was a great man and a great teacher, and that his views and beliefs are truly great, but thats all. Obviously for this to be true he would have to be a lier and therefore a hypocrite, but i believe that the good he was doing and the message he preached was more important than even the ten commandments.

I also believe that the Old Testament is merely a collection of storys from throught acient history (storys which science can prove to be possible, if not definate), which were collected together and, because humans have allways strived for a reason to everything, were changed somewhat to give the impression that these natural frieak occurances were the act of some higher power, who was later known as god.

This theory cannot really be argued against, because the only evidence you have for the bibles truth is the bible itself.

Imagine we were still in the time before civilization. Take the Tsunami for example. This wiped out millions of people, over hundreds of miles. Thousands of years ago these places would be poulated by tribes, and these tribes may have no knowledge of other tribes or anything of the world around them. The few survivors from a tribe would have wondered why they had been wiped from existence so easily. They would not want to think that it was an utterly random occurance, so they might have thought 'it must be God'. Then the survivors would have passed this story down for generations, until it is so central to their society that they cannot possibly comprehend the idea that it was utterly random. This is where the bible came from. A series of 'miraculus' events that have been accumulated over the millenia and turned into a book, and a religion.

There are many arguments against religion and God, but i think the most simple one that no-one really thinks of is 'How can we believ in a book when no one has proof that any of the events actually happened?' Over the centuries religion turned from a believ into a tradition, that was handed down forcebly to the next generation. It is true that people who have abusive parents are more likely to become abusve themselvse. Surely it is the same for religion. The human need for an explination to the unexplainable, combined with the peer pressure of generations, made religion allmost unarguable. Until today, when the free flow of information globally makes NOTHING free from argument.

#170362 48 2006-05-01T06:43:56Z brocerius
Hey, this is random, but it is kind of connected. Do you know how fast we are travelling? A few years ago i work out an estimate:

We are spinning on our axis, orbiting the sun, the sun is orbiting the galaxy (it has been round 18 times so far), the galaxy is moving towards the local cluster, and the local cluster is moving towards the Virgo Supercluster, and everything is moving away from the centre of the universe.

All these speeds, added up, make a total of around 2million mph. However to be traveling that fast we would have to be revolving and orbiting in the same direction, so its fairly unlickely. Also no-one knows how fast we are moving from the centre of the universe so i couldnt add that.

Anyway, i thought it was pretty interesting.

#174686 5 2006-05-11T21:36:18Z brocerius
Any of you heard of the Demographic Transition Model? It shows the birth rates and death rates of countrys during various stages of developement.

Stage 1 High birth rates and high death rates. Few countrys are still here.

Stage 2 Medicine drops the death rate, but the birth rate is still high. This creates a population boom. This is happening in most 3rd world countrys.

Stage 3 Death rates stay low and birth rates drop dramatically. The population levels out. Many 3rd world countrys are moving into this stage.

Stage 4 Birth and death rates are low and stable. Population remains stable. Most 1st world countrys are here.

Stage 5 Birth rates slowly drop below death rates, and population goes into decline. Countrys like Britain are starting to enter this stage.


So basically when the whole wolrd is in stage 5 the problem will be underpopulation, not overpopulation. And the world is moving in that direction. All the countrys that are in stage 4 or 5 have been through all the other stages, and 3rd world countrys are catching up all the time.

Also the EU estimates that with progresses in technology we are now capable of feeding at least 20 billion people. Obviously this will increase with time.

This is why i dont worry about over population.


However, medicine is a problem since antibiotics will soon be inneffective against most bacteria.

#176139 255 2006-05-15T14:36:18Z brocerius
Logic is an absurd tool. You can use logic to prove anything.

For example; it is logically impossible for someone to shoot something. For the bullet/arrow to reach the intended target it would have to pass through an infinit number of units of measurement. Because something infinite never ends the arrow would never reach the target. Of course, in reality we know its possible to shoot something, but it is still logically impossible.

Thanks to 'Jumpers' by Tom Stoppard for the theory.



Im just pointing out that logic is not a very useful tool in arguments.

Quote: Keron Cyst, 2006-05-20T06:14:38Z
What proof does that give of any universal implosion occurring?
The idea of the 'Big Crunch' is a decent way of explaining the big bang. The universe collapses then expands. Repeatedly.

However the 'Big Crunch' will only happen if there is enough mass in the universe for the gravity to pull it back on itself. If there is not enough mass the universe will expand for the rest of eternity.

Scientists dont know how much mass is in the universe, so they dont know what will happen.

2. If you want to practice, unlock all the levels with cheat mode and play 99-4. It is the easyest level.

3. If nobody rates your maps on Numa it is probably 'cos they havnt noticed them. Resubmit them over and over again (at least several thousand times per map) so people can see how great they are. The more times you resubmit them the higher people will rate them.

4. If you want to unlock super-mapmaker mode, go into userlevels and delete the line below "IMPORTANT: changing the following line in any way will screw everything up. Don't do it!!". This message is just to scare of n00bs. Dont listen to it.

5. Highscores are based on the amount of times you die in a level. If you want one just stay in it and keep dying for a couple of days.

6. Whenever you get to the fourth level (level '3') of an episode you must turn off N. This will save your data and help you gat a highscore.

7. When you download N you are agreeing to pay half your salary for the rest of your live to M&R.

8. On the forums the coolest people have high post counts. To get one just keep posting gibberish in all the popular topics. Dont worry if people flame you; its just a test. Keep going anyway and eventually they will stop.

9. For the first week of using the forums you MUST have your caps-lock on all the time. If you dont do this you will be banned.

10. To truly become a master you must follow every bit of advise in this topic. Even if it contradicts itself.

#181225 18 2006-05-28T01:07:16Z brocerius
Quote: LittleViking, 2006-05-26T21:00:16Z
No one can create a triangular circle, that's impossible.

That really depends on what your talking about. In theory it is nearly impossible to creat either a triangle or a circle, since atomic particles would have to be lined up etc. Any triangly we draw is merely a representative of a triangle.

The same is true for a circle. If we presume that a circle is an omnigon (because it has an infinite number of sides) and any shape drawn by us is also an omnigon due to imperfections, then it is arguable that a triangle is a cirlcle (or at least an oval). Because no object is perfect, how do you discriminate between them?

#192312 4 2006-06-21T02:13:52Z brocerius
Cool! A poetry thread!

I used to write loads of peotry at boarding school. Here is the first proper one i wrote, written about two years ago.

The Road Few Travel

I move my eyes up to the sky,
And stare as darkened clouds float by,
An ocean vast above me floats,
The birds pass like unfriendly boats.

A hole bursts, and through it streams
The suns hot light, in violent beams,
And screaming down they pierce my skin,
Destroying any hope within.

But hope or not, I struggle on
In vain, praying that when Im gone,
All the things I left behind
Will seal me in their heart and mind.

But, gone forever, I must seem
Out of their thoughts, beyond a dream.
A shadow on the path diminished.
The road few travel, none finish.


And i like this one. I wrote it about how false Valentines day is. It was written about two years ago as well.

Draft: Valentine

Roses defiantly.
Theyre always good.
Comparisons?
My love is like a.

No.
Far too clichd.
Chocolates?
Maybe not.
Might read as insult.

Ok,
What about love?
Thats always good
Once or twice.
Yes.

My love, vast as oceans, shines bright,
Singing out through dead of night

Forgot to add roses.
Damn.

What about
My blood blossoms from the wound youll make,
If my heart you chose to break
No. Too Gory.

Maybe
Love I feel and show to you,
So will a bunch of roses do?

I think not.

Possibly
Love I feel and roses give,
because with you Id like to live

Far too clumsy.

No,
I think
For another year,
My love is still
Like a bloody red red rose.

Heres another of mine.

I love you oh so much he screams
Staring blindly at delinquent skies
As blood like syrup gently streams
From the vacuum which once held his eyes

The crystal swan, now shattered, fell
From hands which wept and bled
As well as eyes condemned to hell
And clothes all stained red.

Then why she cried, oh why did you
Through love or hate or jealousy
Plunge deep into your eyes, and through,
The swan that you once gave to me.

Then bending down, she gently took
The shattered fragments of the swan,
And clenched her tired fists that shook
Through pain that stabbed her on and on.

Through sightless pain he stumbles on,
And feels her lying at his feet
He sobs I fear our lives are gone,
I know the end Im soon to meet.

Then as blood drips onto her hand
And from there drips to the earth,
They lie within this empty land
Bereft of love or warmth.

The warm, red, life-blood slowly pools
Into one congealing mass
And loveless death soon finds the fools
Who bled into the grass.

#262062 38 2006-11-30T14:24:41Z brocerius
This is a Sestina i wrote. Before you say its crap and doesnt make much sense please take a moment to realise how hard it is to write in this style. Obligatory link for those who dont know what a sestina is; Sestina @ wiki

Sestina


Oh dearly do I love a man
Who wanders as a demagogue.
Who finds, whats more, that he can
Speak clearly through the lies of fog.
Who makes his grace, surveys his plan
For trampling a sinful synagogue.

I kneel outside the synagogue
And speak with an ancient beggar-man
About this life, this world, this plan
Made public by my demagogue.
His mind is filled with death and fog.
He bids me walk. I find I can.

I walk awhile, I find I can
Not face this friendly synagogue.
I'm lost in lies, lost in the fog.
I seek the aid of a crippled man.
Im alone! Help me, my demagogue
Build me, your pawn, into your plan!

I say again; use me! Don't hide your plan.
I know you will, I know you can.
I love my lonely demagogue
He is my sacred synagogue.
And more than that, more than a man
He holds the lies, held in the fog.

Yes! Captured in the dizzy fog
You call your might, unveil your plan
You are but one! One holy man
And you alone, if any can,
Will beat the sinful synagogue
Will become the masterful demagogue.

Your life was fraught, my demagogue.
Now rest your head in ancient fog.
You crushed the sinful synagogue
With me, your servant, in your plan
You found I was your only man
And if you could, you knew I can.

I am your man, your demagogue
I alone can master the fog,
The lies, the plan, the synagogue.
#12597 1 2006-12-02T23:52:35Z brocerius
Disclaimer: For the sake of this argument i will be assuming that the 20-billion-year heartbeat theory is correct; that every big bang is followed by a big crunch and vica versa.



At the end of a universe, after the big crunch, all the energy in the universe is compressed into one point. Because it is so compressed it is not unreasonalbe to suggest that the energy could take the same form as it did in the previous life/s of the universe; that after each compression the energy ends up in the same position. If this is so then we could also presume that the energy would expand in exactly the same way in every one of its infinite expansions, and that the universe is therefore on a loop.

Which brings me to my main point; if the universe is indeed on a loop, as i have hypothesised above, then the life each of us leads has happened before an infinite number of times, and will continue to happen for an infinite amout of time.

So basically our lives last for an infinite amount of time, since they are repeated exactly for all of eternity, which makes us immortal.


Of course, all this depends on a number of unprovable assumptions and physics that i have no idea about, but it is still nice to think that every beautiful sunset, and every wonderfull moment actually lasts for eternity, rather than the fleeting portion of your life that it seems to.


And with that i bid you have a pleasant argument.

#266028 31 2006-12-07T16:58:13Z brocerius
@ AMLT

If every atom, every piece of energy, is expanding in the same way everytime there is a big bang (as hypothesised by me above) then:
a) The circumstances would be exactly the same. Every thought, every action, every movement of an electric charge that creates thoughts and memorys, they would be the same.
b) The universe would compress at the same point every time, because gravity would pull it back at the same point etc.

If you look at it that other way, that the unniverse repeats itself infinitly, but not necesarilly the same each time, statistically our 'version' of the universe would be repeated an infinite number of times, but the things you said would also happen an infinite number of times (or would they? Im not actually sure; maybe the universe has a definite cycle, that changes only in its content rather than its length).

Hope that answers your question




And that word thing is pretty strange. I wonder if i can say cumbersome, or cockerel. How far does the filter go? Its pretty cool, but presumably would become tedious after a while.

#13067 1 2006-12-22T17:28:02Z brocerius
Firstly id like to say sorry for starting another religious thread, but i was bored in the mandatory school church service today and thought of this. This thread is not to argue about the existance of god, so please dont do that. I was just wondering why we exist. Let me elaborate;

If you believe, like i do, that there is no 'free will'; that our thoughts are dependant solely upon our upbringing, surroundings and our genes, then you must agree that there is no such thing as a 'bad' person. 'Bad' people are merely the conglomeration of a number of the mentioned factors, not because they are simpley evil - basically im trying to say that no one is born evil.

And if no-one is born evil, they are merely made so by there surroundings, then surely any actions they make on earth do not reflect on them, but on the person or situation that made them 'bad'. Of course this creates a chain, every bad person being made that way by someone or something before him, and so on, in which case surely it is unfair to blaim enyone for their 'sins'.

Basically im saying that yes, people do bad things, but ill bet that they had no choice (of course they had choice in the normal sense of the word, but the way there minds had been developed would ensure that they made a particular one of the possible choices, therefore negating their ability to choose), and if they had no choice i dont believe they should be punished for it.

Hopefully this will stand up against any arguments that life is a 'test', because life is actually more of a factory; we do not battle our way through it, doing this and that, we actually are pushed through it, having things done to us which makes us the people to do things to others.

And if this is correct, if life isnt really a test, and if every person is actually 'good' at 'heart' then surely life is pointless?

That brings me to my main point; what is the role of life? Why are we here?

Well, i have a theory about that, two;
1 Maybe life is like a factory, and every 'soul' lives through it over and over again, until life finally treats them good enough for them to become good enough to enter heaven, which is why heaven is so awsome; it is full of only the best people from life.
2 Maybe 'life' is actually heaven; maybe each person, when they die, is placed into a world like this and life through it joyfully over and over again. Everyone knows life isnt perfect, but to be honest with you im pretty happy with mine, and for all i know this could be my heaven. After all, there is no evidence that anyone other than me actually exists. Maybe each other member of this suppposed 'world' dosnt actually exist; they are merely here for my eternal enjoyment.




Anyway, that is my thought of the week. Hope you liked it.



And merry christmas/appropriat-politically-correct-multicultural-holday.

Its surprsing how many aspiring poets there are out there... Id allways presumed it was quite unusual. Its nice to see othere peoples poems though. Its rare that you will be exposed to less-than-utterly-brilliant peotry - i mean most of us will only ever see the poetry of nobel prizewinners or poet laureates and stuff like that. Its cool to see normal poetry as well.

Anyway, heres one from a series of poems im working on.


Today I fell into the sea.
How deep, how deep! I ever sink.
Every fathom more tight and free.

My lovers reach the seaside each
Astride a creature, bound and gagged
Delighting in my servility,
Delaying my surrender.
Into the deep they bound like sheep
Noises frothing from their mouths.
Go back to shore! Live as before!

Crowning all there sits the wieght,
Round my laurels it is wreathed,
Of nights in summer with fair Tess
Who even now is not betrothed.
Down to my heart it has bean wreathed.

Suppose I sink beyond their reach;
Under miles of lonely sea
Roaring only at the beach
Roaring, roaring over me.
Obscure defences they'll retain
Understanding they'll neglect.
Now leave them there; do not explain,
Damaging their supposed intellect.
Sometime I will return, they claim.

My mission now will set me free,
Even though I surrender myself to the sea.

#321001 49 2007-05-14T02:04:41Z brocerius
Sincholdrichea; a word which makes sence when written upside down. Made up of Sincholdrichs

Sincholdrich; a letter that can be read upside down. If it reads as a different letter then it is known as a 'Phelutive' Sincholdrich. Examples - 'w' and 'm' are corresponding Phelutive Sincholdrichs, while 'o' or 'I' are simply Sincholdrichs.

#334680 47 2007-06-23T01:12:49Z brocerius
Quote: DemonzLunchBreak, June 04, 2007 03:04 am
Quote: brocerius, June 01, 2007 06:03 pm
That depends on weather you belive in free will or not. I dont. And i belive that without free will we can have no intent - that all the things we supposedly try and do are purely because our environment etc has made us want to 'try' and do them.

I dont believe in intent, unless you could count a bullet that hits someone as having intent. If you belive that bullets are evil for kiling people, then fine. But otherwise, i dont see why you should make up different rules for humans merely because of our supposed 'sentience'.
Are you denying human sentience? Because... uh... we still have that.

Sentience

noun
1. State of elementary or undifferentiated consciousness
2. The faculty through which the external world is apprehended
3. The readiness to perceive sensations; elementary or undifferentiated consciousness

Anyways, in a system of morality that I'm just sort of working on formulating, the difference between the bullet's predictable, determinable behavior and that of a human is just what you've mentioned - awareness of the act. Morality, in the system that I'm formulating, applies to fully conscious, sentient, and intelligent beings.

Morality applies to an action whether or not a being chooses to do it or not. Morality applies because the being is aware of its actions, and because those are the actions that it wants to do, whether influenced only by predictable laws of the universe or not.

I'll make a post about my system of ethics later, when I have more time and feel a little more up to the task. I'm kinda tired right now (be warned - my ideas might be influenced by that).

First, can i just say sorry for not replting sooner. Kind of forgot about this and just remembered today when i saw you arguing with Obby in my thread in BnT.

Anyways, yeah. Basicaly i am saying i dont believe in sentience. Sure, you can pull your pleasant little dictionary definitions and claim it as true, but i mean it in a more... meaningfull way. Yeah, i beleive we are concious, but i dont think that that conciousness is grand enough to deserve any kind of merit. I dont think we are sentient in the grand way people mena when they say it - as if through our sentience we are transported beyond the meagre realm of the atomic, or the ignobility of the innanimate object.

Sure, we move about, we gather our sensory receptors, we communicate things to others of our genre, but i just dont see how that makes us special. All our compulsive, irrational activities are just that - ther is no 'logic' behind peer bonding, there is no great design behind the intricacies of our socable nature. I see these things are mere aspects of our being. Just as a snowflake is majestic, magnificent and beautiful (in as much as these things can be said to exist, of course ) so are we. But I dont think we have anything to do with our own magnificence, just as a snowflake has nothing to do with its.

I dont see how awareness makes that much of a difference. If something is beyond our controll, then surely our awareness of it does little to change it. If we are aware we are doing something evil, but can do nothing to stop it, then surely it is not evil? And if we notice ourselves doing good and can do nothing to prevent it, then surely it is not good?

I think it fundamentally comes down to what you said here -
Quote: DemonzLunchBreak, 2007-06-04T02:04:06Z
because those are the actions that it wants to do
I do not believe that we can 'want' to do anything... i believe that all our uges and dreams are purely dependant upon our upbringing and environment, and as such cannot be said to truly be 'our' urges at all.

Basically, i dont believe in the self. I beleive 'we' are merely an amalgamation of an infinite number of other things, rather than a thing unique on its own. And i dont think that anything that is purely constructed out of fragments of other such creations can be said to have anything as arrogant as a 'soul', nor can it therefore be possesive of something like 'free will', and nor can it be said to be in any way responsible for its actions.

I think the actual point we are arguing has become somewhat diffuse, but i think that should have about covered it.

However since most of it is idle speculation and/or purely based on opinion, i think most of it is good enough. Happy retorting .

#337486 4 2007-07-01T15:40:09Z brocerius
You could deem me ignorant is claiming that Harry Potter was in every way a rip off, but not in claiming it is in any way a rip off. There are numerous aspects of the book that are taken directly from TLOTR, including many characters, and other less tangible aspects. Sure, it is presented in a modern form, and has a differnet time-scale and there are other base aspects that have been edited, but the influence is clearly there, in copious amounts.

Of course, i dont think this in any way de-values the book. Tolkien himself ripped-off huge passages of Beowulf for his book. Ripping off older boks is an integral part of the telling of any story.

#16508 1 2007-08-01T17:54:53Z brocerius
AQA is a text service - you text them a question and they text you the answer. Yesterday i texted this -
Quote: me @ to AQA
Does this sentence make sense - 'Buffalo buffalo Buffalo buffalo buffalo, buffalo Buffalo buffalo.'?
And they replied
Quote: AQA
AQA: No, the sentence does not make sense. There is an inconsistency in the capital letters with no relevant punctuation to accomodate them.

Now, im pretty sure im right, so the next day (today) i texted
Quote: Me @ to AQA
Yesterday i asked "Does this sentence make sense - 'Buffalo buffalo Buffalo buffalo buffalo, buffalo Buffalo buffalo.'?" and you responded "AQA: No, the sentence does not make sense. There is an inconsistency in the capital letters with no relevant punctuation to accomodate them.", however i think you failed to take into account the fact that 'buffalo' can be the name of a city, and therefore would be capitalized. So i ask you to respond again with this in mind, and also remebering that 'buffalo' can also be an action. Thank you.
And, after 38 minutes (which is an unbelievable amount of time for AQA) i recieved this
Quote: AQA @ 38 minutes later
AQA: Sorry. Buffalo (New York) buffalo (animals) Buffalo (NY) buffalo (animals) buffalo (verb: bully) buffalo (bully) Buffalo (NY) buffalo (animals). Phew.

w00t!

Lol. Still, its fairly cool - i defeated a massive corporation (im pretty sure its owned by Virgin) plus managed to get AQA to say 'Phew'. That made my day. Just thought id share .

(BTW, anyone else ever use one of these? Either AQA or something of that ilk?)

They even made a point of stipulating that i would wear school uniform. They claimed that since i had signed this shite, they would kick me out if i varied from it by one iota. So of course, i signed, qnd of course i ignored every word. And they didnt say a fucking thing. In a school of 900 or so i was one of the few who never wear school uniform. And its like it is invisible to all the teachers. Im not a problem kid or anything - i am very popular with all my teacherts, and all that jazz. I just fucking annoys me when the go through the whole fucking pointless rigmarole of making me make promises that not only does no-one expect me to keep, but nobody fucking cares if i dont.

Tits.

#17159 1 2007-09-09T22:06:12Z brocerius
There are two ways of looking at it, aye emm oh. It is well known that beautiful people are good people (with exceptions, obviously, but the rule remains), simply because everyone treats them better. People are more politly, generously, and friendlily inclined toward the more attractive. Thus, they become good people (becuase people learn by example).

So, the question (that im posing) is; is their beauty, in terms of personality, deserved? Does the fact that they are naturally physically beautiful, and are therefore more likely to be personalitily beautiful, negate the fact that they are beautiful?

I suppose you might call it a form of bitterness (not that i am particularly ugly, i am just in no way beautiful), but i just loath the fact that genetics can predispose someone to, quite simply, an easier life. It is a fact that beautiful people get jobs easier, that beautiful people get more lenient sentences (and are, in fact, even brought before judges less often), that they get treated better, get better friends, &c. Basically, if your genes are constructive to a beautiful person, you will have a good life.* Does that annoy any of you?

Much as i hate to bring religion into this (actually, thats a lie - i love to bring religion into everything) i must say that that is one of my own personal proofs against god - beauty. That beauty exists, somehow for me negates the existance of a truly benevolent being. Pain, i can understand (if not condone), but beauty... is just so unfair. Its such a base, cruel, underhand way of God saying to someone 'hey, do you want a rediculously easy life? okay, here - have some super genetics'. Pain is generally circumstantial*, but beauty is allways appreciated. Bastards.

Of course, there is the other way of looking at it - that beautifull people are pre-ordained to be better people, and the beauty they are given is merely the way god achieves this - this may sound the same as the one above, but what i mean is, that maybe God needs these people to be beautiful for some reason or other, and thus makes them genetically beautifull, to help ensure that. Rather than them becoming genetically beautifull, and then good as a unforseen side-effect of this.

Of course, if we take god out of the equation again (for which some of you may thank me), we see this - a genetic heirarchy or brilliant, beautiful people who are generally succssessful, and 'the rest'. They are differnetiated by chance, and any idea of 'deserving' is rediculous, therefore any idea that we should feel jealous of them is rediculous...

I dont know. Its just i was watching some YouTube videos of a girl called 'existentialistcat' (Tsukatu knows who im talking about, if no-one else), and was struck first by her beauty, and then by the fact that my opinions of her views and her videos were deeply and irrevocably skewed by the fact that she is clearly (to me at least, allthough i have no doubt that most would agree with me) extremely beautiful. I felt shitty for knowing that i might think the views of someone more worthwhile, simply becasue that someone is more beautiful. And if we look at it on a larger scale, keeping YouTube as our example, we see the same everywhere - lets take Tsukatu as out starting point. He is widely regarded as an attractive guy on the forums. Take his YouTube friend, dendrophilian - another attractive sort of fellow. Are the view and opinions of these people more valid because they are better physicall specimins of humanity? Are they more important than the ugly people that say equably relevant things on YouTube vidoes?

I think not, and i think that for the sake of fairness, all people on YouTube should have to wear the full body clothing that Arab women wear. That would sort the world out.**

Edit:
Oh, and the links -
http://www.youtube.com/user/existentialistcat
http://www.youtube.com/profile?user=dendrophilian

---------------------------------

*Yeah, generalizations, but generally based in some form of fact. If you truly feel like these generalizations need to be disputed, go ahead, but id like to think they are fairly true.

**Well, obviously not. Im just saying, things are a bit fucked up when we value the thoughts of the attractive more than the thoughts of the unnatractive. And yeah, there is little we can do about it, but that dosnt mean it should go unnoticed. Thoughts and opinions should be viewed at base value, not at the value of the presenter. As dendrophelian said in 'ad homenim' - argue my opinions, dont attack my looks. Well, by the same token, we should value his opinions, not his looks. And the same goes for existentialistcat. Quote: epigone, September 12, 2007 06:27 pm

1984 and Brave New World are both dystopian societies, not utopian.
Quote: brocerius, September 12 @  2007 05:03 am
*In your opinion*

And i entirely disagree.
Also in the opinion of most literary critics. I suppose you could view them as utopian, but it's generally understood that those books are supposed to be a sort of warning about how the future could turn out... consider the context of the novel's, when they were written, the various opinion's and beliefs of both Orwell and Huxley. Read other books by them, you'll see similar themes popping up. They were meant to be dystopian novels.

You take the opinions of literary critics seriously?!! Jesus....

What the books are supposed to be and what they are are two entirely different things. And dont condescend me, i have read many other books by Orwell, allthough admittedly not many by Huxley. You didnt hear me saying that Animal Farm was utopian, did you? You idnt hear me say the Orwell wrote this books proclaiming how great the future was going to be, did you?

Both the books are similar to what Tsukatu said - the whole chip in the brain thing. Utopianism is allmost impossible with freedom. Humans need to be forced to live happy, communist lives. And not just by forcing their actions, but by forcing their thought. It may not make sense to you, but it does to me.

Look at Shallow Hal. Bad movie, but makes a decent point - if you think someone is beautiful, then what does it matter what everyone else thinks of her? If she is beautiful to you then thats fine. Screw what everyone else thinks. Its the same with Brave New 1984 - in both the people are forced into thinking that everything is super-dooper. If we look on it as outsiders, and say "Shit, look at those poor, brainwashed sheep", what does it matter? Ther lives are happy and fullfilling. Thats all it really boils down to.



And now i think about it, how do you know that Orwell didnt think it was utopian? The last thing that is said is, pretty much, "I love my life." He loves Big Brother. He loves the world he is liveing in. That is how it ends. And if someone happened to read only that line if the book it would be like "Aww, he is happy. Thats lovely." It is only because you have read it all that you know he had to suffer incredibly to get to that stage, but the fact he gets there in the end is the only important fact, bacause now that he loves Big Brother he can explain and excuse any of the actions that Big Brother had to do to get him to that state of mind. He can thank Big Brother for making him into a mindless prole.

@Dave, yes an age needs to be arbitrated, but as of now it has not. And if we are looking at this as a court of law would look at it, 15 is the age of consent in some countries. 11 is not.

And, while i agree that the chance of an 18year old being unaware of the implications of sex is slim, it does happen. And vica versa, in the case of 11 year-olds.

Quote: blue_tetris, 2007-09-20T06:07:30Z
Should I be allowed to have sex with a eleven-year-old girl who says "yes"? Is she in a mental condition where her consent is legal?

So, the point i was trying to make; except in excessive cases, such as 11 year olds and younger, it is nigh-on impossible to determine definably wether or not they are in a position to give their consent.
And the same applies to drunks
- At what age can they give consent? At whatever age the law requires.
- At what degree of drunkeness can they give consent? At whatever degree they convince the judge is appropraite.
Now, the social benefits of being alloud to have sex with 11-year olds are infinitly small compared to the damage done to individuals, and therefore society as a whole.
The social benefits of having sex with someone in a possibly dubious mentall state are, again, nill, but in this case the freedom (though it is a word to be weary of using in such a situation) of sexuality implies that any possible/supposed/purported/or, occasionally, actual damage done would be... not neccesarily enough to require such attention as it gets.
Delicate enough?

(And some of you may take issue with the idea of comparing damage and benifits as if this is merely a statistical exercise, but remember that there are people who would be damaged by sex even up to or over the age of 18 - it is impossible to take everyone into account and therefore some semblance of a balance must be struck, and this can only be done by looking at the people involved objectivly.)
#367876 8 2007-09-27T20:11:15Z brocerius
I voted no, but that is entirely irrelevant; swearwords are entirely subjective - this one, for example, may be a swearword to a religous person and not an atheist, and even then it would change in vulgarity depending on the fervourance and acuteness of the religous person in question.

It would be relativly easy to argue that by this definition the *ahem* 'C word' is not a swearword - it does not take the lords name in vain, or anythin like it. But clearly it is defined quite distinclty as a swearword.

So yeah; it depends who sais it, who they say it to, and (allway most importantly), what tone they say it with.


So, to reiterate; to me and you it is not a swearword, to others it is. If it is a swearword to some it is a swearword by definition, therefore the word is, in fact, a swearword.

(So i voted incorrectly ).

#18768 1 2008-01-08T22:12:33Z brocerius
I dont know if you have any examples where you live, but for me there is an obvious one;

Drug dealers.

In Beijing all the 'aggressive' (as opposed to passive) drug dealers are black. By this i mean that the drug dealers who are on the streets or in bars and clubs activly pushing drugs are black. There are white people dealing drugs in Beijing, but they deal only to those who know them, and they dont try and get sales from strangers.
I have never ever witnessed a low-level chinese drug dealer. Obviously the chinese do most of the growing in china, but they sell it on in bulk to non-chinese. They never involve themselves in the actual distribution to drug-takers.

It seems to me that this situation is caused by discrimination, and discrimination is caused by this situation - catch-22 stlye.

Let me explain - blacks currently dominate the market on drug dealing (specifically nigerians, but other african nationalitys work for them too), and anyone who wants to buy drugs in Beijing will talk to a black person to do it. This means that if any other races of people try and sell drugs, they will have less succsess than blacks, because people wont immediatly presume they are dealers. They will have to find all the sales themselves, rather than some coming to them.

Also, the Nigerians controll the specific areas that are known to drug takers - particular bars are used solely for the purpose of selling and taking drugs, and in these bars it would be impossible for a non-black to sell.

Thirdly, Nigerians activly bring in other Nigerains (generally under false pretenses) and coerce them into sellng drugs. No-other race currently does that in Beijing.


This, clearly, causes the government to discriminate against blacks - it is extrememly difficult for a nigerian to get a visa for more than one month, for example, and occasionally they will do a raid on the particular areas in beijing where drugs are sold, focusing wholly on blacks.

Example.



Are situations like this common? Or is Beijing, as the capital of the worlds most advanced third-world country, extermemly bad for it?

And, more importantly; Do you think that the fact that blacks are controlling the drug market in Beijing means that it is fair.. no, reasonable to commit such clearly discriminant acts as happened in the drug raids? Do you think there is any other way the government could deal with the problem?

Personally i think they need to stop supply before they have a chance at stopping the street sellers - for a week or so after the raids you wouldnt see a black face in Sanlitun (the main drinking area), but now there are as many drug dealers as before, albeit mostly new faces.

They deported a handfull of dealers (generally under immigration charges - most of the Nigerains sell their passports when they arrive), but more are arriving all the time.
And unless they stop the drugs from arriving in Beijing, then no matter how many dealers they arrest, they will never stop them from returning in stength.



---

BTW, the people of Bejing are not at all racist - on the very rare occasion that they see an african they treat them as a sort of specatacle, but not in anything of a negative way. It is just the police and government that have problems with them.

Becasue the governement so clearly discriminates against blacks it becomes commonly beleived that all blacks sell drugs. Therefore people who are looking for drugs will immediatly go to a black person to try and get them. This means that not only is it easier for blacks to sell drugs in general, but also it helps to pressurise Nigerians that are brought in under false pretenses to sell as well.

Something along the lines of "Everyone thinks we sell drugs allready, so we might as well."

This discrimination not only supports the black drug dealing community, but also curbs the non-black dealing - if you saw a non-black dealer you would be far more suspicious of them, not to mention the fact that they would have to approach you in the first place, which is more dangerous.


Admittedly this discrimination is only prevalent in the areas of nightlife - in the universities and tourist areas this does not exist.

#426211 39 2008-04-08T05:56:39Z brocerius
Quote: scythe33, April 08, 2008 01:42 am
Quote: brocerius, April 07, 2008 02:19 am
Quote: beginner2, 2008-04-06T05:18:46Z
Anyway, after that about communism, I think that Tibet should be owned by Tibet. It's got its own culture, its own history, its own government.

Erm... so does every other state in China. Couldnt you also say the same about each of the United States? Why do any of those things make Tibet special from any other region of any other country in the world?
Because people in other parts of China don't light themselves on fire?
Because Tibet, unlike most other parts of China (ignoring Xianzhang, because those people seem rather happy at the moment), is separate racially, religiously, and linguistically from China?
Emm... it is statistically probably that many many chinese people set themselves on fire every year. Accidents in the kitchen and so forth... what of it?

Im afraid im going to have to ask for some support for these spurious claims about Tibets individuality.

Racially? China is made up of several different ethnic groups, separated vaguely into four autonomous regions. Within these regions the ethnicality of the people is so different that they are often sub-divided into smaller ethnic groups. Tibet has a rather bland racial individuality, compared to many of the other provinces and regions. China is not a country - it is an amalgamation.

Religiously? Which religion - Confucianism? Taoism? Buddhism? Each of these is spread throughout the region as through the rest of China. Not to mention the 40 million christians and 20 million muslims in China, who can be found from the steppes of Mongolia to the steps of the Forbidden city, to the streets of Tibet.

Linguistically? Well, in my experience (having studied chinese at universitys in both Beijing and Xi'an) there are differences from one city to the next that are formiddable, let alone from each of the provinces to the rest. The regional differences that a non-speaker may term 'accents' allmost constitute different languages, and within China there are four fully appreciated different languages - Manadarin and Cantonese being the most known examples. These are about as disparate as one Asiatic language from another - or one Latinate language from another.

As I said - none of these things make Tibet special above any other of the four autonomous regions in China, nor above many of the individual provinces.


Maybe you people are right though - maybe Tibet should be free. It does have that one defining difference above any other region in China - it really really wants to be separate. But it isnt going to happen. At best it'll become another Taiwan - a 'country' that is allowed 'independance' as long it never has the gall to actually declare it.

As far as the Chinese are concerned Taiwan, Macao, Hong Kong and Tibet are all very much part of China. And untill someone convinces them otherwise, any region that gets uppity will see several hundered thousand PLA troops descending on their country with very little finesse and a great deal of determination.


So; good luck Tibet. You need it.

#19889 1 2008-04-11T05:56:34Z brocerius
I was passing through Laptopia today, when who did I happen to come across but a ninja! He rather stood out from the crowd, being about two meters tall, bright teal coloured, and made wholly of triangles, and at first I was almost too scared to go up to him. When I finally plucked up the courage to say hello he was quite friendly, and he agreed to give me an interview later in the day. The rest of my day seemed to drag on, and I waited listlessly for work to be over. I couldn't wait for the interview - I knew this was a great opportunity for me, and I was not a little apprehensive.

When, just before three o'clock, I was sitting in a caf at the local desktop I felt I had been jilted - it was mere seconds before three, and the ninja was nowhere in sight. Ninja's are known for their punctuality, and I knew if he wasn't there at three he wasn't coming. Suddenly, just as I was about to give up hope, I saw an indistinct shape leap from the stack of shortcuts opposite me - a towering collection of Microsoft Word pieces, where the administration of Laptopia worked daily - and fall, with practised ease, towards me. For a moment I felt scared - I knew that was too great a height for the ninja to survive. He rushed to the ground, and I had to close my eyes before he hit it. When I opened them, instead of the bloody mess I expected to see, there was a ninja standing, whole and hearty, in front of me.

'Sorry for scaring you' he said, with a smile 'shame you had to close your eyes - that was rather a nice jump I just made off the kerb.'

Phew. I forgot that ninjas have the inexplicable ability to cancel out all previous momentum simply by jumping off a wall, but even if I had remembered I wouldn't have thought he would make one so small - I realised this must be a rather exceptional ninja. No noob had I lucked into here.

I ordered a coffee, and he said he would have a hot chocolate. 'Coffee makes me hyper, so I tend to explode when I drink it' he said, smiling again. 'Shall we start?'

He had a friendly smile which he used often, and all my apprehension of the day vanished quickly. I decided to get a little background on him, so asked where he grew up - 'I come from a little IBM in the middle of nowhere. You won't have heard of it - the town was so small when I was a kid that the local council couldn't even afford to upgrade past Onepointone. Not that it made much difference to us - me and my friends used to get a thwump down to the local Version every day after school and play for hours. It was great - we were only noobs then, but we used to get a whole group together and run around Motherlode like maniacs. Technically you were only allowed to play the levels one ninja at a time, but nobody cared back then.'

So you started your training on Onepointone? 'I wouldn't call it training, heh. We were just kids, messing around, having a laugh - but yeah, I guess it put me in good stead for later, when I joined some local competitions and started playing for real. That isn't where I started playing though - back when I was in primary school we didn't even have a local version! Some of the older kids would jump a thwump heading to Ebaumsville, where you could play for free anytime, but kids weren't allowed to go on them without an adult, and my mother never approved of me playing. To be honest, I never like Ebaumsville - there's something inherently shitty about the place. And my parents would rarely take me there anyway - they always told me that I should finish my studies before I started playing, that it was important to learn the theoretical side of The Way before doing it practically, that being killed over and over again was bad for a developing mind and so on A bore, I can tell you. I was a bit lucky though - my dad had a secret passion for the game, and he used to let me come down to the basement with him if I finished my homework. He had quite a nice collection of NKRs down there, and he used to rig them up into wild levels, and send me into them. He didn't mind me getting killed - 'character building' he used to call it. We had a few floorguards in the back garden too, and I used to go out and play with them in the evenings. Those little buggers used to chase me till they were blue in the face! I soon learned how to outsmart them though, and the sight of me being zapped 50 feet into the air, which so alarmed my mother, soon became a thing of the past.' He finished with a hearty laugh, and took a sip of his hot chocolate. It was too hot, apparently, because he fanned his tongue daintily and put it back down. 'I think I'm going to have to nip round the corner for some gold - there's a top-up point just 30 second sprint down the street, so I won't be long. Sorry - I didn't have a chance to grab enough to last me before I came here. If I run out I'll end up back at home, feeling like an idiot, so I better get going.' With that he disappeared from sight with a corner kick off a corner that was, to all extents and purposes, not there. I began to wonder just how good this ninja was...

I was pleased with how the interview was going so far - so many fascinating things to ask about! He seemed to enjoy talking about his past, and was very open. I presume his confidence comes from having an infinite number of lives, and a frankly astounding strength. When you know you could crush the life out of most living things with a flick you probably don't need to be afraid of them - but I never felt in the least afraid of him. I know how strong the pacifist emotion is in the adepts of The Way, and this ninja clearly knew it backwards. His speed, his agility, his precision! It would be terrifying in anyone else, but with ninjas you feel at ease with their power. Its almost a tangible force, radiating from them; I feel stronger already, simply from basking in it!

I looked at my notepad - scribbled in the margins were 'NKRs - Ninja Killing Robots?', 'Thwumps - how dangerous?' and 'Ask about Ebaumsville!' I had plenty to work from, and his talk of competitions had whetted my appetite for more. I took a sip of my coffee, and laughed to myself at the memory at his effeminate tongue-fanning. An amusing little reminder of how human they actually are, beneath the steely muscles and titanium joints.

A teal blur was soaring towards me along the street, feet barely touching the ground. He arrived and deposited himself with a smile and a satisfied sigh. 'I feel like I've collected a million dollars! That was refreshing. What was I saying?'

I asked him about the thwumps, and the danger involved in riding them. This earned me another laugh, as he said 'Dangerous? Maybe to a toddler, but as long as you learn not to get to close to the blue side the things are pretty harmless. A friend of mine got crushed by one once - he fell asleep taking a slow one back from Ebaumsville, and when it got to the depot he woke up just in time to be minced! He reincarnated all the way back at the Ebaumsville Version, and had to get on another! Personally I don't like the slow ones. Don't have the patience. I'd rather take a runner, and get there in half the time!' A runner? 'Sorry - colloquial. The fast movers - the attackers, you might call them, even though they are almost purely functional nowadays - are called runners, because you've got to keep running to stay on top. I have no idea why - someone tried to explain it to me once, but it was too technical. You can either take them, or one of the walkers - a retreating thwump, in other words. They are more casual, and we ninjas can sleepwalk pretty easily, so some people take them if they are looking for a nap on the way home.'

I had heard about this phenomenon - since ninjas are so much more active than humans, their relative state of 'rest' might be called a leisurely walk for us.

I thought we would move on from childhood, so I asked what he did after he graduated. 'Actually, I didn't. Failed my exams, never got the theory down. By the exam years I had become a little obsessed - there is nothing in the world like the thrill of tap-dancing over mines, or stroking a passing rocket. Nothing compares to falling for a whole map, just to bounce of a locked door, into a tunnel, and back up to the roof before you can say 'Sweet Jesus that was fast'. Really, I became an adrenaline junky - I was tired of my small-town life and looking for something big, so I packed up my belongings, stole a bunch of gold from my parents, and legged it to Ebaumsville before I even took my exams. I had to go on foot all the way - I knew they would look for me on the thwumps - so it took me a while. By the time I arrived I was desperate, really low on gold. At one point I was on the street, begging for just a few pieces from passing strangers, living from second to second. If I died I would go straight to one of the Ebaumsville reincarnation spots, and I knew they would be being watched. I had to stay alive, so I joined some of the underground tournaments there, playing desperate games in illegal Versions. It wasn't satisfying, but it kept me going. And the fact that I couldn't let myself die meant that i had to become really good, really fast. The amount of levels I went through... unbelievable. I could never do it all again, not without dying at least a couple of times. But back then... I had such unbelievable force of will, I just kept on racing, kept on dodging those lasers and skipping between bullets - before long I became a legend in the underground rings. Ninjas would come from all over the 'Net to challenge me, and I defeated every one.

'Until, at last, came my dying day. I had been playing for weeks, throwing down competitor after competitor. It was rumoured that I was invincible, and by that time I had enough hubris to start thinking it might be true. But there were other rumours growing - a new Version had come out, and there were daring players springing up all over the place, some almost as desperate as me. I grew complacent, so when I heard that a young player, known only as Trib4l, was starting to dominate his way through the rankings I wasn't in the least afraid. Until he set me his challenge. It was only this; 'MTI.'

'MTI was new, unplayed. It had been finished by one of the most feared mapmakers of the time, one who had nearly killed me on many an occasion, and I had been secretly afraid of going for it. Everyone was desperate to see me do it - my friends, because they believed it would be my greatest run. My enemies because they believed it would be my undoing. I had been avoiding it for weeks, making up flimsy excuses. But now, it was unavoidable. I steeled myself, and set to training. I did mine-jumpers for days, ceaselessly. I became almost untouchable, bouncing off the ground on the pixels between mines, without dying once! It wouldn't bee believed if you saw it today, but back then there were no speedhacks, no cheats. We were raw, we were young, and we were dangerous. And finally, the day arrived. It was to be played in my Version - the Version I was champion over, the Version I had defeated so many young hopefuls in. It was to be my most glorious day - or his.

'We entered MTI. Neither of us had seen it before - and neither of us was ready for it. The first corridor stretched ahead, liberally bedecked with mines, glinting evilly from the floor. The spikes and straits of the walls looked dangerous, and the jumps we would have to do - formidable. And I knew this was only an inkling of the full horror of the map. I knew there would be many corridors, and many dangers ahead. I had heard rumours of course... such horrifying rumours, but I couldn't bring myself to believe them. Not until I saw it. But once my eyes had lain upon this first corridor, I knew that the worst was true. I knew thwumps and falls, mines and traps, spikes and slopes and death, death, death was ahead, waiting for me to greet it with a fresh, unknown fear in my heart. So little gold... so far to go...

'I looked to my left, at Trib4l. I felt the fear show itself in my face, and looked desperately for a trace of it in him. He smiled, the starting gun fired, the crowds screamed, and my long learned legs threw me forwards.'



I sat, enraptured by this god, this demon in front of me. His skin had grown dark, black, and I knew he had reverted to his true colour, that he had gone back, within himself, to that terrible time. His eyes were dark, and they stared unseeing into me, as he told of the journey through those perilous paths.



'We soared, side by side, over the first mines. Our hands reached out instinctively and stole the gold from the air, our legs pumped mercilessly at the tips of every spike. We barely touched a slope, flying from peak to peak. I was dazed, by the beauty and evil of the map, by the strength and agility of my adversary, and by the unfathomable speed with which we sped along the first corridor. We flew, together, under the drone, squeezing past its electrifying tendrils, its static making our bodies quail in remembered fear. We glided over the mines following, suspended by nothing but our determination. We flew towards the thwump, kicked oft its corner as it dumbly attacked what wasn't there, and fell. As we soared downwards, a moment of peace came upon me. Here was a moment of repose, a time to gather my shattered confidence and prepare for the onslaught ahead. I saw the thwump below me, and knew what I had to do, and knew that Trib4l knew it too. A shared moment passed between us, and for a second we were allies, not enemies. We were pioneers, legends, destroying this level together. The upcoming thwump passed us soundlessly, and sped towards the roof. It meant nothing to us, and our minds were focused once more on our landing, on setting the next thwump in motion and sprinting along with it. The ground met us, our angles were perfect, and at once we were sprinting towards the left wall. Gold was captured, the approaching thwump was dodged, and we were kicking our way up the leftmost corridor. Here, we were perfect. Our rise was swift; from ledge to ledge we leapt in a frenzy of pixel-accuracy that no future Faneca, no clux or naem would ever surpass. This was an unrecorded beauty, a perfection that would see no submission into the ranks of supposed heroes. This was excellence, and as we embarked upon the next corridor we knew that every witness here today would take something unique with them for the rest of their lives.

'I was too numb with energy to think, and the fear of the start had been replaced with the freedom, with the euphoria of knowing nothing but that your feet will guide your path. The Way was strong in me now, and if I had thought at all it would have been with a glimmer of hope that I could make it. But Trib4l was beside me, and his feet were as sure as mine, and his mind was as much a blank. He had joined me in the mindless plateau of perfection, and if ever I gained a pixel on him he would take it back in milliseconds. The thwump was no object, and I soared past it unhindered, smashing my fist into the glory of the exit switch. For a moment I was alone, and a flutter of joy crept into me - had he been caught? Had the thwump conquered my rival? Joy almost took me, until I saw a black figure accelerate past - he had let the thwump squeeze him! He had caught a boost of acceleration that I had missed! For the merest of seconds I almost failed - my feet missed a kick and I slowed to a mere sprint. I saw him drop down the next fall, saw the blackness in his eyes as he fell, and almost lost hope. My mind had lost its plateau, and I heard the roars of the crowd pressing into my ears, pounding my body. I was inches from abandon when I reached the fall, but I had been handed a gift from my young enemy - the thump he triggered had almost reached the top, and I dived forward with one last hope in me. I hit the bottom left corner of the rising thwump with such speed and precision that I was thrown down the fall, and onto the right angle that waited me with such speed as to almost kill me - my knees buckled for a moment, and bent.

'My eyes caught his shape ahead, delayed for the merest moment by the coming thwump, and my bending legs straightened to propel me on. I soared over the thwump, caught him, kicked off the next corner and I was ahead by a mere handful of pixels. I felt his anger hit me like a wave of pain, but I was back into my mode and nothing could stop me. My feet were flying, my mind set, and my form perfect. I could feel him pushing, smashing his feet off any and every available jump, but I was there before him and I caught them all too. We reached the next rise, and I knew this was the last - knew that one corridor lay ahead, and at the end of that was everything. At the end of the next corridor lay my life, my hopes, my future and past and present and all that I would ever need was to get there. The end of the next corridor meant eternal glory, and I would reach it. I hit the top corner with him close behind me. I smiled to myself, already assured, and glanced back at the challenger. A smile was set to take my face, but when I looked back he was not there. For the meanest moment I looked for him, uncomprehending, and then my eyes were back on the path. Except, there was no path. I searched, my eyes fought to see a way, my mind searched desperately, but I knew I could not make it - I was going too fast, I had abandoned myself to the foolish hope that there would be a way along this corridor, so I had dived headlong into it, but there was no route, there was nothing ahead of me but the blue glare of a thwump, glad to have trapped me at last. I hit the gap in the mines desperately, hoping beyond hope that I would be able to pass this monster, but long before I even pushed off the ground I knew it was too late. The world slowed, swirled, and became nothing but a great blue square, rushing towards me, with some daring tendrils of electricity already leaping for my fragile body. One caught me, the world flashed white, and I was gone.'



I breathed once more, the air coming out ragged and chocking. My heart was racing, and my pen lay abandoned on the table, next to two cold drinks and a notebook. He seemed deathly, as if he was truly reliving it. He shuddered once, and then a pale, blue glow seeped over his skin.



'He told me later than he knew. He knew the last corridor couldn't be approached with such speed, so he had slowed himself, and gone over carefully. He didn't need to rush then - he saw my body strewn in pieces about the last hall. The mines had been cleared by me, so he walked gently through it all, and stepped into the door. A glorious win, and I do not grudge him for it - I had defeated many a beginner on levels I knew. He was truly an excellent ninja.

'He invited me to join him. He was moving out of the underground, going to Metanet Forum, the ninja capital. I declined, but also left the underground. Ebaumsville is no place to live - a dirty, decrepit city. Rife with corruption, and the people there are frankly detestable.'

He smiled a little, trying to regain his lightness. I asked if he went on to play again.

'Of course,' he really smiled this time. 'I couldn't leave the game just because somebody beat me. The Way teaches you not to worry about winning to much, as long as you lose beautifully. I had begun to forget the teachings, in my madness in Ebaumsville. I am almost glad that Trib4l beat me. I think he saved me from myself, in a way.

'I moved to the New Mountain Arena - known as NuMA - and continued playing. It's an amazing place - a gathering of all the greatest mapmakers and players, working together to perfect The Way.'
I had heard of NuMA, from my studies into The Way, and felt honoured to be in the presence of one of its greats. I asked what was happening there now.

'There are always loads of things happening, new maps to be played every few minutes, and the greats are still there, mapping and playing away. There has been an influx of new players since the latest Version was developed, and its great to see so many young minds at work.'

I thought it was about time to wrap up the interview, but I had one or two things I wanted to ask before I went, firstly about the new craze that was sweeping the ninjas - religion.

'Hah, yeah, a lot of the new players are getting into that. Superstition in my opinion - The Way is religion enough for me. But a lot of the young ninjas are buying into it. It's the belief - how shall I put this... I think its ridiculous, but basically it is the belief that there is some form of Higher Power controlling our lives. That we are merely at the whim of some higher being, that everything we do, every map we complete and every step we take, is guided by His or Her Fingers. They believe that when a run is going well it is because They are Well Fed, Warm, and Comfortable. I think those are the three main supposed causes, although I won't pretend to be particularly knowledgeable on the subject. Also, when a run is going badly, or when you continue to make stupid mistakes on a level, it is because they are Uncomfortable, Hungry, or Their Attention is Otherwise Engaged. There is also something about having Cold Fingers, but I can't recall exactly what. I think it is supposed to make your reactions slower, or something. They also believe that each of us has his own personal guide, a single entity that directs our feet, and that some are better than others, which is why some ninjas are more adept than others. I don't hold much stock in the whole thing - I believe that my successes are my own, and no-one else's. But they don't do any harm by it, so I say let them have their stupid ideas.'

And lastly I wanted to know something about his personal life - does he have any romantic interests at the moment?

'Well, actually, I am married. I've got a lovely wife, called Alice, and two little ninja babies. I met Alice back in Ebaumsville - she was one of the hopefuls I defeated in my time. The story from there is long and complicated, so I wont bore you with it. Suffice it to say we are happily married, and bent on bringing up two of the next generations best players.'

With a smile, he produces a photo from... somewhere, and shows it to me - it is of him and a lovely blue ninja, who I am told is Alice, and their children, both black, called Jim and Bexley. I congratulate him, and thank him for the interview.

'It was my pleasure. If you are ever out and about, fell free to drop by my house - ISPN 126.5634.2342. Our local council has finally got a grant for the next Version to be put in - we are still on Onepointone back home - so when Onepointfive comes out we can maybe get together and have a run about MTI, for old times sake. I'll show you some of those jumps, if I can still pull them off.' With a smile and a leap, he disappears.





So, there you have it. An interview with one of NuMA's greats, and a champion of old. Hope you enjoyed it.

Spoiler: Original Content of PostIn my opinion any decent organisation should have a reading list. So lets make one for Metanet!

Firstly, do you think the books should only be related in some way to Metanet, or just good books? I'd be for the latter, but a little of the former would be good too - The Ninja, for example, is a pretty good book that is clearly related.

So, if you have a book you would like to add to the reading list, suggest it, and unless its rediculous I'll add it to this post. If anyone then has a problem with any of the books on the list, say so and they can be contested in this thread. Sound acceptable?

And, once we have enough books and enough agreement, we can have a finished list.


I'll whack up a few randon, good (IMO), books here to start it off. Contest or agree as you will. And what sort of format do you think it should be in?

#438004 79 2008-05-21T08:45:58Z brocerius
Okay, apologies for being away for a while.

Thanks for all the suggestions, and feel free to keep them coming, but now i think we can start to do a bit of whittling.

For the whittling, i dont think by any means that we should only remove books that we think are bad or unworthy, but also books that are a little unnecesary, or which dilute the point a little - for example, i think there are too many Kurt Vonnegut book. I like Kurt Vonnegut, but i think that if we narrow the suggestions to manybe three, they will be more likely to be read.

On the subject of Kurt Vonnegut, however; I think he would be a good example for an author to put in the Authors of Note section (which is as yet uncreated).

Authors of Note is an idea which i have been toying with for this list for a while - where there is a particularly poular author, i think a little piece should be written about him and his style of writing, and maybe a few suggestions for people who want to read his work.

I think Roald Dahl should go in that list as well, and ill try and write an example piece for him - i dont know enough about Kurt to do one for him.


Roald Dahl is a renowned writer, known mostly for his Childrens fiction (James and the Giant Peach and Charlie and the Chocolate Factory being two notable examples), but who had also shown his skill in childrens poetry (Revolting Rhymes, for instance) and in writing more macabre collections of adult literature (Skin and Kiss kiss). His autobiography (in two parts - Boy and Going Solo) is also a worthwhile and captivating read.
Roald Dahls style is exciting, funny and very touching on occasion, while being known for its grousome but childish violence. He is a writer that has captivated readers of all ages since he started writing, and his timeless masterpieces are a must read for any fan of literature.


So what do you think? Good idea? Good enough execution?


And get whittling!

#450174 29 2008-07-02T10:47:33Z brocerius
I really dont like this constant reptition on the theme 'If people didnt have guns, they would kill you with something else'.

Firstly, as South Park said, "You cant run from a .44. Go ahead and try." Guns are ranged weapons, which are rare today. And arrows are dodgable at range.

And secondly there is the simple unnavoidable fact that it is a great deal harder to kill someone with a pillow than with a gun. There are literally billions of people in the world who could kill me with a gun, but probably only a handful who could, in the same situation, kill me with a pillow. If i had to choose between everyone having a gun and everyone having a pillow, im pretty certain i know what i would choose.

And the same, to an admittedly lesser degree, goes for knives, bottles, rapiers and ninja stars. It takes effort. It takes time. It takes skill. It dosnt take the twitch of a finger.

Why is this Great Leveller neccesarily a good thing? Does the world somehow become more awsome when everyone could kill everyone else with the same ease?
